Full answer
-----------

Based on the UFLPA Entity List updates published between November 25, 2024 and August 3, 2026, the entities that were first added during that window and mine, process, or source copper are:

- Xinjiang Nonferrous Metals Industry Group Co., Ltd. (added Nov. 25, 2024 — mines, smelts and processes raw metals including copper)
- Xinjiang Habahe Ashele Copper Co., Ltd. (also known as Ashele Copper) (added Jan. 15, 2025 — mines and produces copper, zinc and silver)
- Zijin Mining Group Co., Ltd. (added Jan. 15, 2025 — global mining of copper, zinc, gold, etc.)
- Baiyin Nonferrous Group Co., Ltd. (added Aug. 3, 2026 — mines, smelts and processes nonferrous/precious metals including copper)
- Xinjiang Baiyin Mining Development Co., Ltd. (added Aug. 3, 2026 — subsidiary that owns/operates mines sourcing copper and molybdenum)

No other entities first placed on the list in that period are described as mining, processing, or sourcing copper.

Full answer
-----------

The entities first added to the UFLPA Entity List between November 25, 2024 and August 3, 2026 that mine, process, or source copper are:

- Xinjiang Nonferrous Metals Industry Group Co., Ltd. (added Nov. 25, 2024) — mines, smelts, and processes raw metals including copper
- Xinjiang Habahe Ashele Copper Co., Ltd. (also known as Ashele Copper) (added Jan. 15, 2025) — mines and produces copper, zinc, and silver
- Zijin Mining Group Co., Ltd. (added Jan. 15, 2025) — mines and extracts copper (among other metals)
- Baiyin Nonferrous Group Co., Ltd. (also known as Baiyin Nonferrous / BNMC) (added Aug. 3, 2026) — mines, smelts, and processes non-ferrous metals including copper (via its Xinjiang subsidiary)
- Xinjiang Baiyin Mining Development Co., Ltd. (added Aug. 3, 2026) — operates a mine in Xinjiang producing copper and molybdenum

So there are five such entities across the three updates in that window.

Full answer
-----------

The entities first added to the UFLPA Entity List between November 25, 2024 and August 3, 2026 that mine, process, or source copper are:

- Xinjiang Nonferrous Metals Industry Group Co., Ltd. (added Nov. 25, 2024) – mines, smelts, and processes raw metals including copper
- Xinjiang Habahe Ashele Copper Co., Ltd. (also known as Ashele Copper) (added Jan. 15, 2025) – mines and produces copper, zinc, and silver
- Zijin Mining Group Co., Ltd. (added Jan. 15, 2025) – extracts metals including copper
- Baiyin Nonferrous Group Co., Ltd. (also known as Baiyin Nonferrous; BNMC) (added Aug. 3, 2026) – mines, smelts, and processes non‑ferrous metals and sources copper from Xinjiang
- Xinjiang Baiyin Mining Development Co., Ltd. (added Aug. 3, 2026) – operates the Xinjiang mine from which the group sources copper and molybdenum

So, in any order: Xinjiang Nonferrous Metals Industry Group Co., Ltd.; Xinjiang Habahe Ashele Copper Co., Ltd.; Zijin Mining Group Co., Ltd.; Baiyin Nonferrous Group Co., Ltd.; and Xinjiang Baiyin Mining Development Co., Ltd.

Entities First Placed on the UFLPA Entity List (Nov 25, 2024 – Aug 3, 2026) That Mine, Process, or Source Copper

1. Xinjiang Nonferrous Metals Industry Group Co., Ltd. — First placed on November 25, 2024 (section 2(d)(2)(B)(ii) list). Described as a company that "mines, smelts, and processes raw metallic materials, including copper, lithium, beryllium, nickel, manganese, and gold."
2. Baiyin Nonferrous Group Co., Ltd. (also known as Baiyin Nonferrous; and BNMC; and formerly known as Baiyin Nonferrous Metals Company) — First placed on August 3, 2026 (section 2(d)(2)(B)(v) list). Described as involved in "mining, smelting, processing" of non-ferrous metals and determined to be "sourcing copper and molybdenum from the XUAR."
3. Xinjiang Baiyin Mining Development Co., Ltd. — First placed on August 3, 2026 (section 2(d)(2)(B)(v) list). Identified as a wholly-owned subsidiary of Baiyin Nonferrous that operates a mine in the XUAR, and determined to be "sourcing copper and molybdenum from the XUAR."

Full answer
-----------

Entities First Placed on the UFLPA Entity List (Nov. 25, 2024 – Aug. 3, 2026) That Mine, Process, or Source Copper

Based on the provided documents, the following entities meet the criteria:

1. Xinjiang Nonferrous Metals Industry Group Co., Ltd. — First added on November 25, 2024 to the section 2(d)(2)(B)(ii) list. Described as a company that "mines, smelts, and processes raw metallic materials, including copper, lithium, beryllium, nickel, manganese, and gold."
2. Zijin Mining Group Co., Ltd. — First added on January 15, 2025 to the section 2(d)(2)(B)(v) list. Described as a "global mining company…principally engaged in the exploration and extraction of metals, including zinc, copper, lead, silver, gold, iron ore, and sulfuric acid," and which "sources material from the Xinjiang Uyghur Autonomous Region."

Note: Xinjiang Habahe Ashele Copper Co., Ltd. (also known as Ashele Copper), which mines and produces copper, was already on the Entity List (section 2(d)(2)(B)(ii)) before November 25, 2024, so it was not "first placed" on the list during the specified period and is excluded.

Full answer
-----------

The list in force on November 15, 2024 was the one published November 1, 2024 (superseded on November 25, 2024).

- November 25, 2024 update (Doc 2) listed 107 entities and added 30 new unique entities (5 to §2(d)(2)(B)(ii) and 25 to §2(d)(2)(B)(v)).

So the November 1 list had: 107 − 30 = 77 entities.

- August 3, 2026 update (Doc 6) lists 187 entities.

Growth calculation:

\[ \frac{187 - 77}{77} \times 100 = \frac{110}{77} \times 100 \approx 142.857\% \]

Rounded to one decimal place: 142.9%.

The high model answers with every document, then once with each document you marked necessary left out. A document is needed when the answer without it is wrong, or changes from the every-document answer, or when it is the only document naming an item you marked it as excluding. Reaching the same answer without it means that document is not needed.

Final answer: ## Final answer | Your answer (gold): 139.7%

Full answer
-----------

Determining the two data points

1. The list in force on November 15, 2024

The notice of November 25, 2024 (Doc. 2) states: "This notice supersedes the UFLPA Entity List published in the Federal Register on November 1, 2024 (89 FR 87391)." So on November 15, 2024 the operative register was the November 1, 2024 list — not the 107‑entity list of November 25. The November 1 figure must be back‑calculated from the November 25 update.

The November 25, 2024 notice reports 107 entities on the consolidated list, and the update added:

- 5 entities to the §2(d)(2)(B)(ii) list (Western Gold Co.; Western Gold Hami Gold Mine; Western Gold Karamay Hatu Gold Mine; Xinjiang Nonferrous Metals Industry Group; Xinjiang Zhonghe), and
- 25 entries to the §2(d)(2)(B)(v) list.

However, one of those 25 (v)-list additions — Xinjiang Daqo New Energy Co., Ltd. — is expressly described as a company that "was first listed on the UFLPA Entity List, pursuant to Section 2(d)(2)(B)(i), in June 2022." It is therefore not a new entity; it is merely added to a second sub‑list.

This is confirmed by the notices' own accounting convention: the Jan. 15, 2025 update reports 3 + 35 + 1 = 39 sub‑list additions yet the total rises only from 107 to 144 (i.e., +37), because Aksu Huafu (renamed) and Xinjiang Habahe Ashele were already listed; and the Aug. 3, 2026 update reports 4 + 41 = 45 sub‑list additions but states "forty‑‑three new entities... two of which appear on two separate sub‑lists." Totals count distinct entities.

So the November 25, 2024 update added 30 − 1 = 29 new entities: 107 − 29 = 78 entities on the list in force on November 15, 2024.

(Verification: the Nov. 25 appendix contains 9 + 38 + 60 = 107 distinct entities; removing the 5 (ii) and 25 (v) additions, with Daqo retained under (i), leaves 9 + 33 + 36 = 78.)

2. The list as of August 3, 2026

Doc. 6 states: "One hundred and eighty‑seven entities... are specified on the UFLPA Entity List."

Calculation

Growth = (187 − 78) / 78 × 100 = 109 / 78 × 100 = 139.7436...%

(For reference, naively subtracting all 30 sub‑list additions to get 77 would yield 142.9%, but that double‑counts Xinjiang Daqo New Energy, which was already listed under §2(d)(2)(B)(i) and so was not a new entity on November 15, 2024.)

Final answer

139.7% (78 entities on November 15, 2024 → 187 entities on August 3, 2026)

Full answer

Calculating UFLPA Entity List Growth

August 3, 2026 baseline: Document 5 explicitly states 187 entities on the UFLPA Entity List.

November 2024 baseline: Document 3 (January 15, 2025) states it supersedes the list published November 25, 2024 (89 FR 92953) and announces the following additions:

- 3 new entities to the section 2(d)(2)(B)(ii) list
- 35 new entities to the section 2(d)(2)(B)(v) list
- 1 new entity to both the (ii) and (v) lists (Xinjiang Zijin Zinc Industry Co., Ltd.)
- 1 technical correction (no new entity)

Total new unique entities added: 3 + 35 + 1 = 39

The January 2025 list contains 144 entities, so the November 2024 list contained: 144 − 39 = 105 entities

Percentage growth:

$$\frac{187 - 105}{105} \times 100 = \frac{82}{105} \times 100 \approx 78.095\%$$

Answer: 78.1%

Reasoning
---------

Step 3 — The November 2024 baseline

Doc. 3 states its appendix "supersedes the UFLPA Entity List published in the Federal Register on November 25, 2024 (89 FR 92953)" — i.e., the list in force in November 2024. That update added:

- 3 entities to the §2(d)(2)(B)(ii) list (Xinjiang Energy (Group), Xinjiang Energy (Group) Real Estate, Xinjiang Jinbao Mining);
- 35 entities to the §2(d)(2)(B)(v) list; and
- 1 entity to both lists (Xinjiang Zijin Zinc Industry) — counted once.

Total unique additions = 3 + 35 + 1 = 39 (the Aksu Huafu entry was only a technical name correction, not an addition).

Therefore the November 2024 list contained: 144 − 39 = 105 entities

(Sanity check: counting the four sub-lists in the Jan. 2025 appendix gives 9 + 43 + 108 = 160 list entries; removing 16 duplicate appearances of entities on more than one sub-list gives exactly 144. ✓)

Growth calculation

$$\frac{187 - 105}{105} \times 100 = \frac{82}{105} \times 100 = 78.095\%\approx 78.1\%$$

Final answer

≈ 78.1% growth (from 105 entities in November 2024 to 187 entities on August 3, 2026).

Calculating UFLPA Entity List Growth

Step 1: Determine entities on the list in force on November 15, 2024

The list in force on November 15, 2024 was the November 1, 2024 list. The November 25, 2024 notice (Document 2) supersedes that list and reports 107 total entities after adding:

- 5 new entities to section (ii) — all entirely new
- 25 new entities to section (v) — of which 1 (Xinjiang Daqo) was already on section (i)

So 29 new unique entities were added: 5 + 24 = 29.

November 1, 2024 count: 107 − 29 = 78 entities

Step 2: Determine entities on the list in force on August 3, 2026

The latest UFLPA Entity List in the provided documents is the January 15, 2025 update (Document 4), which states 144 total entities. No further updates appear in the provided documents through August 2026.

August 3, 2026 count: 144 entities

Step 3: Calculate percentage growth

$$\frac{144 - 78}{78} \times 100 = \frac{66}{78} \times 100 = 84.615\ldots\%$$

The number of UFLPA-listed entities grew by 84.6%.

Full answer
-----------

107 − 30 = 77 entities

The list published on August 3, 2026 (Document 6) states that 187 entities are specified on the UFLPA Entity List.

Growth calculation:

\[ \frac{187 - 77}{77} \times 100 = \frac{110}{77} \times 100 \approx 142.857\% \]

Rounded to one decimal place:

142.9%

Full answer
-----------

\[ \frac{187 - 77}{77} \times 100 = \frac{110}{77} \times 100 \approx 142.857\% \]

142.9%

Full answer

Calculating UFLPA Entity List Growth (November 1, 2024 → August 3, 2026)

Step 1: Determine the count on November 1, 2024

The November 25, 2024 notice states it supersedes the November 1, 2024 list and reports 107 total entities after adding:

- 5 entities to the §2(d)(2)(B)(ii) list (all new: Western Gold Co., Western Gold Hami, Western Gold Karamay, Xinjiang Nonferrous Metals Industry Group, Xinjiang Zhonghe)
- 25 entities to the §2(d)(2)(B)(v) list

Of the 25 (v) additions, Xinjiang Daqo New Energy Co., Ltd. was already on the §2(d)(2)(B)(i) list — so it is not a new unique entity. This yields 29 unique new entities (24 from (v) + 5 from (ii)).

November 1, 2024 count: 107 − 29 = 78 entities

Step 2: Determine the count on August 3, 2026

The August 3, 2026 notice states: 187 entities are specified on the UFLPA Entity List.

Step 3: Calculate growth

$$\frac{187 - 78}{78} \times 100 = \frac{109}{78} \times 100 = 139.74\%$$

The number of UFLPA-listed entities grew by approximately 139.7%.
